## Summary Report

I've thoroughly explored the Minecraft-inspired voxel game's item, block, and recipe systems. Here are my findings:

### 1. **Where Items & Blocks Are Defined**

**Blocks:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/blocks.ts` — Contains `BlockId` enum (50 block types including air, building blocks, ores, furniture) and color palettes (`BLOCK_COLORS`, `HELD_BLOCK_COLORS`)

**Items:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts` — Contains `ITEM_DEFS` array (80+ items), `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID` lookup map, block-to-item mappings (`BLOCK_TO_SLOT`), and break hardness metadata

**Recipes:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts` — 54 crafting recipes + 10 villager trades, all in a flat `RECIPES` array

### 2. **Categorization Metadata Already Present**

Items have a **`kind` field** (from `ItemKind` type in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts`, line 4):
```typescript
export type ItemKind = "block" | "weapon" | "tool" | "armor" | "food" | "material";
```

**Other metadata per item:**
- `maxDurability` — tools, weapons, armor (non-stackable if present)
- `minePower`, `mineTier` — tools only (pickaxe hierarchy)
- `attack`, `meleeReach`, `throwDamage` — weapons
- `armorSlot`, `defense` — armor
- `hunger` — food items
- `blockId` — which block this item places

**Recipes structure** (`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts`, lines 43–53):
```typescript
export type Recipe = {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  cost: Array<{ slotId: string; count: number }>;
  result: { slotId: string; count: number };
  station?: "furnace" | "villager";
};
```

The `station` field already gates recipes (furnace/villager only), but there is **no category/group/tag field** on recipes themselves.

### 3. **How Item Icons/Sprites Are Generated**

**Procedural sprite generation (no asset files):**

- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/ui/spritePixels.ts` — Generates all 16×16 pixel-art inventory icons at runtime:
  - **Block items**: Auto-rendered as isometric cubes using `BLOCK_COLORS` palette
  - **Tools** (pickaxes, hoes): Painted from shape grids (`PICKAXE_GRID`, `HOE_GRID`, etc.) + material color ramps (`MATERIAL_PALETTES` — wood, stone, sliver, ruby, sapphire, gold, diamond, dragon tiers)
  - **Weapons** (swords, spears, knife): Painted from shape grids + same material palettes
  - **Food/materials** (wool, coal, food, arrow, etc.): Painted from custom grids + palettes in `ITEM_SPRITE_GRIDS`

- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/atlas.ts` — Generates the world texture atlas (block faces) procedurally with per-block noise and accent details (ore sparkles, furnace glow, chest latch, etc.)

- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/ui/sprites.ts` — Wraps pixel buffers into canvas data-URLs (cached, DOM-optional)

### 4. **Documentation on Adding Content**

**Key file:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/adding-content.md`

**For blocks** (step-by-step):
1. Add to `BlockId` enum and `BLOCK_COLORS` in `blocks.ts` → atlas auto-generates tile
2. Add `BREAK_HARDNESS` entry in `items.ts`
3. Make placeable: add `ITEM_DEFS` entry (kind: "block", blockId) + `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` mapping
4. Optionally add recipes
5. Non-cube/non-solid blocks need engine work (collision, meshing)
6. Map to sound family in `lib/game/audio/materials.ts`
7. Add lighting class in `lib/world/lighting.ts` (opacity, emission)

**For items/recipes** (line 16–28):
- Add to `ITEM_DEFS` with appropriate `kind` and stats
- For tools: `minePower`/`mineTier`/`maxDurability`
- For weapons: `attack`/`maxDurability`
- For armor: `armorSlot`/`defense`/`maxDurability`
- For food: `hunger` value
- For materials: just `kind: "material"`
- Add inventory sprite in `spritePixels.ts` (auto-generated for material-prefixed tools, custom grids for others)
- Recipes: `{ id, label, cost: [{slotId, count}], result: {slotId, count}, station? }`
- Optional `station: "furnace"` or `station: "villager"` gates the recipe

### 5. **Recipe Categorization/Grouping**

**Currently: NONE.** 

Recipes are a flat array displayed in `InventoryPanel.tsx` (lines 154–180) with no grouping, tabs, or categories. The recipe book shows all available recipes in a single scrolling list.

**Only existing filtering:**
- Station-gated: `station: "furnace"` or `station: "villager"` hides recipes until that station opens
- UI disables recipes you can't afford (via `canCraft` check)
- UI switches label "Crafting" ↔ "Trading" depending on `craftingStation`

**The codebase already groups recipes at render time by:**
1. Filtering by `station` property in the UI
2. The 54 crafting recipes are internally organized in comments in `recipes.ts`: "Building & materials", "Tools", "Weapons", "Ranged & endgame", "Armor", "Food & smelting"

But these are **code comments, not data structure**, so the UI has no way to query them for grouping.

---

## Recommendations for UI Categorization

To group crafting recipes in the UI, you could:

1. **Add a `category` field to `Recipe` type** (parallel to the existing `station` field):
   ```typescript
   station?: "furnace" | "villager";
   category?: "building" | "tools" | "weapons" | "ranged" | "armor" | "food" | "other";
   ```

2. **Update `ITEM_DEFS` entries with item categories** so you can derive recipe categories from result items (e.g., result is "diamond_pickaxe" → category "tools")

3. **In `InventoryPanel.tsx`, group recipes by category before rendering** — either as sections within the recipe list or as tabs.

The `kind` field on items already provides a coarse categorization (block/weapon/tool/armor/food/material) which covers ~70% of the grouping you need; a second-level category on recipes or items would let you split weapons (melee vs. ranged), tools (gathering vs. farming), and building (decoration vs. functional).

## Summary

I've completed a thorough exploration of the crafting system in the Monecraft voxel game. Here are my findings:

### 1. Crafting Table UI Components

**Main Component:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`

The crafting interface is a single component called `InventoryPanel` that serves as the survival inventory dialog. It has two distinct columns:

- **Left column (inventory):** 
  - Chest grid (if chest is open) — displays up to 27 slots
  - Main inventory grid — 27 slots arranged 9×3
  - Hotbar row — 9 slots
  - Armor column (left side) — 6 armor equip slots

- **Right column (recipe book):**
  - "Crafting" or "Trading" heading (dynamic based on `craftingStation`)
  - Recipe list panel with scrollable recipe entries

**Styling:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/app/ui.css` (lines 238-407)
- `.inventory-panel` — main container with Minecraft-style 3D beveled border
- `.recipe-book` — flex column with border-left separator
- `.recipe-list` — grid layout, max-height 360px with overflow-y auto
- `.recipe-entry` — each recipe shown as a flex button with ingredient icons, arrow, and result icon

### 2. Recipe Definition & Storage

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts`
**Trades Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/trades.ts`

**Total Recipes:**
- 54 crafting recipes (lines 4-441 in recipes.ts)
- 10 villager trades (lines 13-50 in trades.ts)
- **Total: 64 recipes**

**Recipe Structure** (TypeScript type from `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts`):
```typescript
type Recipe = {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  cost: Array<{ slotId: string; count: number }>;
  result: { slotId: string; count: number };
  station?: "furnace" | "villager"; // optional station requirement
};
```

**Recipe Categories by Usage:**
- **Base crafting table recipes:** 45 recipes (planks, bed, tools, weapons, armor, etc.)
- **Furnace recipes:** 9 recipes (charcoal smelting, cooking raw meats)
- **Villager trades:** 10 recipes (split into 4 SELL trades for materials→emeralds, 6 BUY trades for emeralds→goods)

### 3. Current Recipe Discovery & Browsing UX

**How it works:**
- Players see ALL available recipes in the recipe book at all times as a vertical scrolling list
- Recipes are displayed in a **fixed order** — no search, filter, or sort
- Recipe entries show ingredient icons → result icon (visual formula representation)
- Recipes are **enabled/disabled** via styling based on:
  - `canCraft()` check — whether the player has the required items
  - Station requirements — if a recipe requires a furnace or villager, it's disabled if not currently using that station
- Disabled recipes appear at opacity 0.45 with grayscale-filtered icons

**Discovery model:** Pure exploration — players must recognize items by icon or hover for tooltips. No recipe book progression, no unlocking, no categorization. The assumption is the player knows recipes or tries random combinations.

### 4. Output Display

**Single output slot per recipe:**
- Each recipe shows exactly one result item (with count)
- Output is dynamically created on crafting via `craft()` function in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.ts`
- The function:
  1. Validates `canCraft()` — checks inventory has cost items AND space for result
  2. Consumes cost items from inventory
  3. Adds result items to inventory (fills existing stacks first, then empty slots)
  4. Returns null if the operation can't complete (all-or-nothing)

### 5. Existing Grouping & Categorization

**In the recipe list:** **None** — recipes are displayed in a flat list with no grouping, filtering, or sorting.

**In the item system** (`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts`):
- 80 item definitions with kinds: `"block" | "weapon" | "tool" | "armor" | "food" | "material"`
- Item distribution:
  - 23 blocks
  - 17 weapons
  - 14 materials
  - 11 food items
  - 8 tools
  - 6 armor pieces

**Station-based gating:**
- 9 furnace recipes (smelting/cooking) locked to furnace use
- 10 villager trades locked to villager interaction
- 45 base crafting table recipes (no station requirement)

**Accessibility features:**
- Hover tooltips on recipe entries showing the full label
- Station-locked recipes show a tooltip explaining "Requires Furnace"
- Disabled recipes are visually dimmed
- ARIA labels on all interactive elements

---

**Key files for understanding the implementation:**
1. `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx` — UI rendering
2. `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts` — recipe definitions
3. `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.ts` — `canCraft()` and `craft()` logic
4. `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/app/ui.css` — styling (lines 238-407)

1	import { useState } from "react";
2	import ItemIcon from "@/components/game/ItemIcon";
3	import { itemTooltipFor, useItemTooltip } from "@/components/game/ItemTooltip";
4	import PixelImg from "@/components/game/PixelImg";
5	import { CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE } from "@/lib/game/engine/commands";
6	import { ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS, ARMOR_SLOTS, createSlot } from "@/lib/game/items";
7	import { itemIconUrl } from "@/lib/ui/sprites";
8	import type { EquippedArmor, InventorySlot, Recipe } from "@/lib/game/types";
9	
10	type InventoryPanelProps = {
11	  inventory: InventorySlot[];
12	  equippedArmor: EquippedArmor;
13	  selectedHotbarSlot: number;
14	  hotbarSlots: number;
15	  recipes: Recipe[];
16	  craftingStation: "furnace" | "villager" | null;
17	  /** Contents of the open chest, or null when no chest is open. */
18	  container: InventorySlot[] | null;
19	  canCraft: (recipe: Recipe) => boolean;
20	  onSwapSlots: (fromIndex: number, toIndex: number) => void;
21	  /** Moves a slot across the inventory/chest boundary (chest indices offset by CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE). */
22	  onMoveStack: (fromIndex: number, toIndex: number) => void;
23	  onToggleEquipArmor: (index: number) => void;
24	  onCraft: (recipe: Recipe) => void;
25	};
26	
27	const STATION_LABELS: Record<NonNullable<Recipe["station"]>, string> = {
28	  furnace: "Furnace",
29	  villager: "Villager"
30	};
31	
32	/**
33	 * The survival inventory: armor column, 27-slot storage grid, hotbar row, and
34	 * a recipe book where every entry shows ingredient icons and the result.
35	 * Items move by clicking one slot and then another (click-to-swap); clicking
36	 * an armor item toggles equipping it instead.
37	 */
38	export default function InventoryPanel({
39	  inventory,
40	  equippedArmor,
41	  selectedHotbarSlot,
42	  hotbarSlots,
43	  recipes,
44	  craftingStation,
45	  container,
46	  canCraft,
47	  onSwapSlots,
48	  onMoveStack,
49	  onToggleEquipArmor,
50	  onCraft
51	}: InventoryPanelProps) {
52	  const [pendingIndex, setPendingIndex] = useState<number | null>(null);
53	  const { tooltip, bind } = useItemTooltip();
54	
55	  // While a chest is open, moves span both grids (chest slots are offset by
56	  // CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE); otherwise a swap stays within the player inventory.
57	  const slotAt = (index: number): InventorySlot | undefined => (index >= CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE ? container?.[index - CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE] : inventory[index]);
58	
59	  const onSlotClick = (index: number) => {
60	    const slot = slotAt(index);
61	    if (!slot) return;
62	    // Clicking armor in the player inventory equips it; chest slots only move.
63	    if (index < CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE && slot.kind === "armor" && slot.count > 0) {
64	      onToggleEquipArmor(index);
65	      setPendingIndex(null);
66	      return;
67	    }
68	
69	    if (pendingIndex === null) {
70	      setPendingIndex(index);
71	      return;
72	    }
73	    if (pendingIndex === index) {
74	      setPendingIndex(null);
75	      return;
76	    }
77	    (container ? onMoveStack : onSwapSlots)(pendingIndex, index);
78	    setPendingIndex(null);
79	  };
80	
81	  const isEquipped = (slot: InventorySlot) => slot.kind === "armor" && !!slot.id && equippedArmor[slot.armorSlot ?? "helmet"] === slot.id;
82	
83	  const renderSlot = (slot: InventorySlot, idx: number, extraClass = "") => {
84	    const isChest = idx >= CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE;
85	    const name = isChest ? `Chest slot ${idx - CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE + 1}` : `Slot ${idx + 1}`;
86	    return (
87	      <button
88	        key={`inv-slot-${idx}`}
89	        className={["inv-slot", extraClass, pendingIndex === idx ? "pending" : "", isEquipped(slot) ? "equipped" : ""].filter(Boolean).join(" ")}
90	        onClick={() => onSlotClick(idx)}
91	        {...bind(itemTooltipFor(slot))}
92	        aria-label={slot.id && slot.count > 0 ? `${name}: ${slot.label}` : `${name}: empty`}
93	      >
94	        <ItemIcon slot={slot} size={32} />
95	      </button>
96	    );
97	  };
98	
99	  const hotbar = inventory.slice(0, hotbarSlots);
100	  const storage = inventory.slice(hotbarSlots);
101	
102	  return (
103	    <div className="inventory-panel">
104	      <div className="inventory-columns">
105	        <div className="inventory-main">
106	          {container ? (
107	            <div className="chest-section">
108	              <div className="inventory-heading">Chest</div>
109	              <div className="inv-grid chest-grid" data-testid="chest-grid">
110	                {container.map((slot, i) => renderSlot(slot, CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE + i))}
111	              </div>
112	            </div>
113	          ) : null}
114	          <div className="inventory-heading">Inventory</div>
115	          <div className="inventory-upper">
116	            <div className="armor-column">
117	              {ARMOR_SLOTS.map((armorSlot) => {
118	                const equippedId = equippedArmor[armorSlot];
119	                const equippedIndex = equippedId ? inventory.findIndex((slot) => slot.id === equippedId && slot.count > 0) : -1;
120	                const equippedItem = equippedIndex >= 0 ? inventory[equippedIndex] : undefined;
121	                return (
122	                  <button
123	                    key={`armor-${armorSlot}`}
124	                    className={equippedItem ? "inv-slot armor-slot filled" : "inv-slot armor-slot"}
125	                    onClick={() => equippedIndex >= 0 && onToggleEquipArmor(equippedIndex)}
126	                    {...bind(equippedItem ? itemTooltipFor(equippedItem) : { title: `${ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot]} (empty)` })}
127	                    aria-label={equippedItem ? `${ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot]}: ${equippedItem.label}` : `${ARMOR_SLOT_LABELS[armorSlot]}: empty`}
128	                  >
129	                    {equippedItem ? (
130	                      <ItemIcon slot={equippedItem} size={32} />
131	                    ) : (
132	                      <span className="armor-ghost">
133	                        <PixelImg src={itemIconUrl(armorSlot)} alt="" size={32} aria-hidden />
134	                      </span>
135	                    )}
136	                  </button>
137	                );
138	              })}
139	            </div>
140	            <div className="inventory-hint">
141	              Click one slot, then another to move items.
142	              <br />
143	              Click armor to equip or unequip it.
144	            </div>
145	          </div>
146	          <div className="inv-grid storage" data-testid="storage-grid">
147	            {storage.map((slot, offset) => renderSlot(slot, offset + hotbarSlots))}
148	          </div>
149	          <div className="inv-grid hotbar-row" data-testid="hotbar-grid">
150	            {hotbar.map((slot, idx) => renderSlot(slot, idx, idx === selectedHotbarSlot ? "active" : ""))}
151	          </div>
152	        </div>
153	
154	        <div className="recipe-book">
155	          <div className="inventory-heading">{craftingStation === "villager" ? "Trading" : "Crafting"}</div>
156	          <div className="recipe-list">
157	            {recipes.map((recipe) => {
158	              const stationLocked = !!recipe.station && recipe.station !== craftingStation;
159	              return (
160	                <button
161	                  key={recipe.id}
162	                  className="recipe-entry"
163	                  onClick={() => onCraft(recipe)}
164	                  disabled={stationLocked || !canCraft(recipe)}
165	                  aria-label={recipe.label}
166	                  {...bind({ title: recipe.label, lines: stationLocked ? [`Requires ${STATION_LABELS[recipe.station!]}`] : undefined })}
167	                >
168	                  <span className="recipe-ingredients">
169	                    {recipe.cost.map((cost) => (
170	                      <ItemIcon key={`${recipe.id}-${cost.slotId}`} slot={createSlot(cost.slotId, cost.count)} size={24} />
171	                    ))}
172	                  </span>
173	                  <span className="recipe-arrow" aria-hidden>
174	                    →
175	                  </span>
176	                  <ItemIcon slot={createSlot(recipe.result.slotId, recipe.result.count)} size={24} />
177	                </button>
178	              );
179	            })}
180	          </div>
181	        </div>
182	      </div>
183	      {tooltip}
184	    </div>
185	  );
186	}
187	
```

> AGENT

I've now got a clear picture of how crafting works today. Here's my honest take before I propose anything.

## What exists now

The "recipe book" (right column of `InventoryPanel.tsx`) is a **single flat scrolling list of every recipe** — 45 base crafting recipes at the table, plus furnace/villager recipes that swap in when you're at those stations. Each row is `ingredient icons → result icon`. There's **no grouping, no labels, no search, no sorting**. Recipes you can't afford or that need a station are just dimmed in place.

Two things make that list hard to scan:
- **It's long** — ~45 rows at the base table.
- **It's repetitive** — 7 tiers each of pickaxe / sword / spear = 21 near-identical rows, so the things you're hunting for blur together.

## My opinion: yes, grouping is worth doing

A few reasons it's a genuine win and low-risk here:
- The data **already carries the grouping signal** — every result item has a `kind` (`block | weapon | tool | armor | food | material`) in `lib/game/items.ts`. So categories can be *derived*, not hand-maintained, which means they can't drift out of sync with the recipes.
- `recipes.ts` is **already loosely ordered by category** in source (the comments literally say "Building & materials / Tools / Weapons / Ranged & endgame / Armor / Food & smelting") — grouping just makes that structure visible instead of implicit.
- It's **faithful to Minecraft** — the modern MC recipe book has category tabs (equipment / building / misc / food), so this isn't a foreign concept; if anything the current flat list is the less-Minecrafty version.

The main caveat: at the furnace and villager the list is already filtered to one station, so grouping mostly pays off in the **base crafting-table view**. That's fine — that's where the 45 rows live.

There are a few ways to present it, and which one you want changes the implementation, so let me check your preference.

> TOOL

211	export const ITEM_DEF_BY_ID: Record<string, ItemDef> = Object.fromEntries(ITEM_DEFS.map((item) => [item.id, item]));
212	
213	export function maxStackSizeForItem(itemId: string): number {
214	  return ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[itemId]?.maxDurability ? 1 : MAX_STACK_SIZE;
215	}
216	
217	export function createEmptySlot(): InventorySlot {
218	  return { id: null, label: "Empty", kind: null, count: 0 };
219	}
220	
221	export function createSlot(itemId: string, count: number): InventorySlot {
222	  const def = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[itemId];
223	  if (!def) return createEmptySlot();
224	  const slot: InventorySlot = { ...def, count: Math.min(maxStackSizeForItem(itemId), Math.max(0, Math.floor(count))) };
225	  if ((def.kind === "tool" || def.kind === "weapon" || def.kind === "armor") && def.maxDurability) {
226	    slot.maxDurability = def.maxDurability;
227	    slot.durability = def.maxDurability;
228	  }
229	  return slot;
230	}
231	
232	export function createInitialInventory(): InventorySlot[] {
233	  const slots: InventorySlot[] = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
234	  const starter: Array<{ id: string; count: number }> = [
235	    { id: "grass", count: 64 },
236	    { id: "dirt", count: 64 },
237	    { id: "stone", count: 64 },
238	    { id: "wood", count: 64 },
239	    { id: "planks", count: 20 },
240	    { id: "cobble", count: 20 },
241	    { id: "sand", count: 20 },
242	    { id: "wood_pickaxe", count: 1 },
243	    { id: "knife", count: 1 }
244	  ];
245	  for (let i = 0; i < starter.length && i < slots.length; i += 1) slots[i] = createSlot(starter[i].id, starter[i].count);
246	  return slots;
247	}
248	
249	export const BLOCK_TO_SLOT: Partial<Record<BlockId, string>> = {
250	  [BlockId.Grass]: "grass",
251	  [BlockId.Dirt]: "dirt",
252	  [BlockId.Stone]: "stone",
253	  [BlockId.Wood]: "wood",
254	  [BlockId.Leaves]: "dirt",
255	  [BlockId.Planks]: "planks",
256	  [BlockId.Cobblestone]: "cobble",
257	  [BlockId.Sand]: "sand",
258	  [BlockId.Brick]: "brick",
259	  [BlockId.Glass]: "glass",
260	  // Coal ore drops the usable coal item directly (not a placeable ore block).
261	  [BlockId.CoalOre]: "coal",
262	  [BlockId.SliverOre]: "sliver_ore",
263	  [BlockId.RubyOre]: "ruby_ore",
264	  [BlockId.GoldOre]: "gold_ore",
265	  [BlockId.SapphireOre]: "sapphire_ore",
266	  [BlockId.DiamondOre]: "diamond_ore",
267	  [BlockId.Snow]: "snow",
268	  [BlockId.Cactus]: "cactus",
269	  [BlockId.Bed]: "bed",
270	  [BlockId.Furnace]: "furnace",
271	  [BlockId.Chest]: "chest",
272	  [BlockId.MossyCobblestone]: "mossy_cobble",
273	  [BlockId.Torch]: "torch",
274	  [BlockId.Tnt]: "tnt",
275	  [BlockId.DoorNorthLower]: "door",
276	  // Tilled soil reverts to dirt; immature wheat returns its seed.
277	  [BlockId.Farmland]: "dirt",
278	  [BlockId.WheatStage0]: "seeds",
279	  [BlockId.WheatStage1]: "seeds",
280	  [BlockId.WheatStage2]: "seeds"
281	};
282	
283	/**
284	 * Items a broken block yields. The default is its single `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` entry;
285	 * grass occasionally also drops a seed (the natural seed source), and mature
286	 * wheat drops wheat plus 1–2 seeds. `rng` is injectable for deterministic tests.
287	 */
288	export function rollBlockDrops(block: BlockId, rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
289	  if (isDoorBlock(block)) return [{ itemId: "door", count: 1 }];
290	  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];
291	  const base = BLOCK_TO_SLOT[block];
292	  if (base) drops.push({ itemId: base, count: 1 });
293	
294	  if (block === BlockId.Grass && rng() < GRASS_SEED_DROP_CHANCE) {
295	    drops.push({ itemId: "seeds", count: 1 });
296	  }
297	  if (block === BlockId.WheatStage3) {
298	    drops.push({ itemId: "wheat", count: 1 });
299	    drops.push({ itemId: "seeds", count: 1 + Math.floor(rng() * 2) });
300	  }
301	  return drops;
302	}
303

{inventoryOpen ? (
  <InventoryPanel
    inventory={inventory}
    equippedArmor={equippedArmor}

    container={container}
    canCraft={canCraft}
    onSwapSlots={swapInventorySlots}
    onMoveStack={moveStack}
    onToggleEquipArmor={toggleEquipArmor}
    onCraft={craft}
  />
) : null}
```

**Props source:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts` (line 341)
- `recipes: RECIPES` — passes the **full RECIPES array** directly, not filtered. RECIPES is imported from `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts` (line 14) and is not filtered based on station or availability.

---

## 2. **Tests touching the recipe book**

**Primary test file:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx`

Current test queries by:
- **`aria-label`**: Tests query recipes via `screen.getByRole("button", { name: "2 Wood -> 4 Planks" })` (line 48), using the recipe's `label` prop as the button's `aria-label` (InventoryPanel.tsx line 165).
- **No direct DOM class queries**: Tests do NOT use `.recipe-entry` CSS class in selectors. They only filter button elements by role.
- **Craft buttons are queried by label/role**, not by test-id or CSS class.

**What will break:**
- If you add section header divs (e.g., `<div className="recipe-section">Tools</div>`) **between** recipe buttons, the DOM tree structure changes but `aria-label`-based queries remain robust.
- **Risk zone:** Any test that counts total recipe buttons via `screen.getAllByRole("button")` might inadvertently count new section elements if they are also buttons. Your section headers should likely be `<div>` or `<span>`, not `<button>`, to avoid this.

**Other test files touching recipes:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.test.ts` — Tests recipe integrity (every recipe references real item ids) and crafting logic. Does not touch the UI, so grouping/sorting won't affect these.
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.test.ts` — Tests config consistency.
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.test.ts` — Tests inventory algebra, not UI.
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.test.ts` — Headless engine tests.

---

## 3. **The `kind` field usage**

**ItemKind enum:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts` (line 4)
```ts
export type ItemKind = "block" | "weapon" | "tool" | "armor" | "food" | "material";
```

**ITEM_DEF_BY_ID:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts` (line 211)
```ts
export const ITEM_DEF_BY_ID: Record<string, ItemDef> = Object.fromEntries(ITEM_DEFS.map((item) => [item.id, item]));
```
Exported as a derived const from `ITEM_DEFS` array. To look up an item def by `slotId`, use `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[slotId]`.

**Helper function to get item kind for grouping:**
```ts
// In your grouping logic, you'd do:
const resultItemDef = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[recipe.result.slotId];
const kind = resultItemDef?.kind; // "tool" | "weapon" | "armor" | "food" | "block" | "material" | undefined
```

**Recipe type:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts` (lines 43-53)
```ts
export type Recipe = {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  cost: Array<{ slotId: string; count: number }>;
  result: { slotId: string; count: number };
  station?: "furnace" | "villager";
};
```
No built-in `kind` field on Recipe. You'll compute it by looking up `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[recipe.result.slotId].kind`.

I have everything I need. Two findings worth flagging that shaped the plan:

1. **The full `RECIPES` array is passed to the panel** (`useMinecraftGame.ts:341` → `MinecraftGame.tsx`), and station-locked recipes (furnace/villager) are *always rendered, just dimmed*. So the flat list is actually ~64 rows at the base table, not 45 — even more reason to group.
2. **Tests query recipes by `aria-label`/role, never by CSS class or button count assumptions** (`InventoryPanel.test.tsx`), so adding non-`<button>` section headers is safe.

One design decision I made for you (and explain in the plan): rather than scatter the furnace/villager recipes across the kind-based groups (which would spray villager trades into Materials/Building/Food), I'll give `station` recipes their own **Smelting** and **Trades** sections. That keeps your chosen 5 kind-groups clean and matches the existing furnace=smelting / villager=trading mental model. Let me write up the plan.

## Approved Plan:
# Group recipes by category in the crafting recipe book

## Context

The recipe book (right column of the survival inventory) renders **every recipe as one
flat, unlabeled scrolling list** — and because the full `RECIPES` array is passed in and
station-locked recipes are merely dimmed rather than hidden, that's ~64 rows at the base
crafting table (45 craftable + 9 furnace + 10 villager, all visible). It's hard to scan
for two reasons: it's long, and it's repetitive (7 tiers each of pickaxe/sword/spear = 21
near-identical rows that blur together).

The data already carries everything needed to fix this: every result item has a `kind`
(`block | weapon | tool | armor | food | material`) in `lib/game/items.ts`, and
`recipes.ts` is already loosely ordered by category in source. This change makes that
implicit structure visible — **category section headers** within the same scrolling list,
with **craftable-now recipes floated to the top of each group**. It's purely a UI
presentation change (no save/worldgen/config impact) and mirrors modern Minecraft's
category-tabbed recipe book.

Decisions locked with the user:
- **Layout:** section headers in one continuous scroll (not tabs, not search).
- **Ordering:** within each group, recipes the player can make *right now* sort first;
  the rest stay dimmed below in source order.
- **Station recipes** (furnace/villager) get their own **Smelting** and **Trades**
  sections rather than scattering across the kind groups — keeps the kind groups clean and
  matches the furnace=smelting / villager=trading model.

## Approach

Derive each recipe's category (station takes precedence, otherwise the result item's
`kind`), group + sort in a small **pure, testable** helper in `lib/game/recipes.ts`, then
render section headers in `InventoryPanel.tsx`. No props or wiring change — the panel
already receives `recipes`, `canCraft`, and `craftingStation`.

### 1. Categorization + grouping helper — `lib/game/recipes.ts`

Co-locate with the recipes (where the category comments already live). Add:

- A `RecipeCategory` string-union type and an ordered list of categories:
  `["Tools", "Weapons", "Armor", "Building", "Food", "Materials", "Smelting", "Trades"]`
  (matches the order the user previewed; station groups last).
- `recipeCategory(recipe: Recipe): RecipeCategory` — pure:
  1. `recipe.station === "villager"` → `"Trades"`
  2. `recipe.station === "furnace"` → `"Smelting"`
  3. else map `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[recipe.result.slotId]?.kind` →
     `tool→Tools, weapon→Weapons, armor→Armor, block→Building, food→Food,
     material→Materials` (fallback `Materials`).
  - Import `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID` from `@/lib/game/items` (no new dependency edges — `items.ts`
    does not import `recipes.ts`).
- `groupRecipes(recipes, canMakeNow: (r: Recipe) => boolean): Array<{ category, recipes }>`
  — pure: buckets recipes by `recipeCategory`, drops empty categories, returns them in the
  fixed category order; **within each bucket, stable-partition so `canMakeNow` recipes come
  first**, preserving source order in each partition. Keep it allocation-light; no sorting
  surprises (use a stable partition, not `Array.sort`).

### 2. Render section headers — `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`

- Build the predicate once: `canMakeNow = (r) => !stationLocked(r) && canCraft(r)`, where
  `stationLocked(r) = !!r.station && r.station !== craftingStation` (factor the existing
  inline expression at line 158 into a local helper so the render loop and the predicate
  share it).
- Replace the single `recipes.map(...)` (lines 157–179) with
  `groupRecipes(recipes, canMakeNow).map(group => …)`: emit a **non-button** header
  (`<div className="recipe-category" role="presentation">{group.category}</div>`) followed
  by that group's existing `.recipe-entry` buttons. Header must NOT be a `<button>` so
  `getAllByRole("button")` counts in tests stay correct.
- The per-entry button markup (icons → arrow → result, `disabled`, `aria-label`, tooltip)
  is unchanged — only its container/order changes.

### 3. CSS — `app/ui.css` (recipe-book block ~363–407)

Add a `.recipe-category` rule: small bold uppercase muted label, a little top margin
(first one no margin), full grid-row width. The `.recipe-list` grid already lays children
in rows, so header divs slot in without layout changes. No change to `.recipe-entry`.

## Tests

- **`lib/game/recipes.test.ts`** (extend) — unit-test the pure helpers: `recipeCategory`
  for one representative recipe per branch (e.g. `stone_pickaxe`→Tools,
  `diamond_sword`→Weapons, `helmet`→Armor, `door`→Building, `bread`→Food,
  `wool_from_string`→Materials, `charcoal`→Smelting, a villager trade→Trades); and
  `groupRecipes` — assert categories come back in the fixed order, empty categories are
  omitted, and craftable-now recipes precede dimmed ones within a group (preserving source
  order) given a stub `canMakeNow`.
- **`components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx`** (extend) — assert the category headers
  render and that existing recipe lookups by `aria-label`/role still pass (regression that
  grouping didn't drop or duplicate any recipe). Add one assertion that, given a controlled
  `canCraft`, a craftable recipe appears before a non-craftable one in the same group.

## Docs + changelog (ship with the change)

- **`docs/manual.md`** — crafting section (~223–233): note recipes are grouped by category
  with craftable ones surfaced first.
- **`docs/reference.md`** — recipe section (~26–28): mention the recipe-book grouping.
- **`docs/testing.md`** — add the new recipe-grouping unit tests to the coverage map.
- **`CHANGELOG.md`** — under `## [Unreleased]` → `### Changed`: "Recipe book now groups
  recipes by category (Tools, Weapons, Armor, Building, Food, Materials, Smelting, Trades)
  with craftable-now recipes listed first in each group."
- `docs/adding-content.md` / `docs/architecture.md`: no change needed — categories are
  *derived* from the existing `kind`, so the Recipe shape is unchanged and adding a recipe
  needs no extra step.

## Git workflow

New feature → its own branch off `main` (per AGENTS.md), focused slices, Conventional
Commit messages with a body explaining the why, no `Co-Authored-By` trailers.

- **Branch:** `feat/recipe-book-categories`
- **Commit 1** — `feat(crafting): derive recipe categories and a grouping helper`
  `lib/game/recipes.ts` (the `recipeCategory` / `groupRecipes` helpers + types) and the
  `lib/game/recipes.test.ts` additions. Pure logic, ships with its tests.
- **Commit 2** — `feat(crafting): group the recipe book by category, craftable first`
  `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`, `app/ui.css`, `components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx`.
- **Commit 3** — `docs(crafting): document recipe-book category grouping`
  `docs/manual.md`, `docs/reference.md`, `docs/testing.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Before the PR:** run the full verify gate (below) and confirm it's green.
- **PR:** `gh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main`; no AI-attribution lines in the
  description (per saved preference).

## Verification

- `bun test` — new + existing unit/component tests green.
- `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun run build` — the CI
  `verify` gate.
- Manual gameplay pass (visual change → per `docs/testing.md`): `bun run dev`, press `I`,
  confirm the recipe book shows labeled sections in order, craftable recipes sit at the top
  of each group, and dimmed/station-locked ones below; open a furnace and a villager and
  confirm the **Smelting** / **Trades** sections become active. (`bun run test:e2e` not
  required — no renderer/input/React-shell behavior change beyond this panel, but run it if
  convenient.)

1	import { describe, expect, test } from "bun:test";
2	import { createEmptySlot, createSlot, ITEM_DEF_BY_ID } from "@/lib/game/items";
3	import { canCraft, countsById, craft } from "@/lib/game/inventory";
4	import { RECIPES } from "@/lib/game/recipes";
5	import type { InventorySlot } from "@/lib/game/types";
6	
7	function inventory(items: Array<[string, number]>): InventorySlot[] {
8	  const slots = Array.from({ length: 12 }, () => createEmptySlot());
9	  items.forEach(([id, count], i) => {
10	    slots[i] = createSlot(id, count);
11	  });
12	  return slots;
13	}
14	
15	const recipe = (id: string) => {
16	  const found = RECIPES.find((r) => r.id === id);
17	  if (!found) throw new Error(`recipe ${id} not found`);
18	  return found;
19	};
20	
21	describe("recipe integrity", () => {
22	  test("every recipe references only real item ids", () => {
23	    for (const r of RECIPES) {
24	      for (const cost of r.cost) expect(ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[cost.slotId], `${r.id} cost ${cost.slotId}`).toBeDefined();
25	      expect(ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[r.result.slotId], `${r.id} result ${r.result.slotId}`).toBeDefined();
26	    }
27	  });
28	});
29	
30	describe("ranged recipes", () => {
31	  test("a bow is craftable from wood + string", () => {
32	    const slots = inventory([
33	      ["wood", 3],
34	      ["string", 3]
35	    ]);
36	    expect(canCraft(slots, recipe("bow"))).toBe(true);
37	    const next = craft(slots, recipe("bow"));
38	    expect(next).not.toBeNull();
39	    expect(countsById(next!).get("bow")).toBe(1);
40	  });
41	
42	  test("arrows craft four at a time from stone + wood + feather", () => {
43	    const slots = inventory([
44	      ["stone", 1],
45	      ["wood", 1],
46	      ["feather", 1]
47	    ]);
48	    expect(canCraft(slots, recipe("arrow"))).toBe(true);
49	    const next = craft(slots, recipe("arrow"));
50	    expect(next).not.toBeNull();
51	    expect(countsById(next!).get("arrow")).toBe(4);
52	  });
53	
54	  test("a bow cannot be crafted without enough string", () => {
55	    const slots = inventory([
56	      ["wood", 3],
57	      ["string", 2]
58	    ]);
59	    expect(canCraft(slots, recipe("bow"))).toBe(false);
60	  });
61	});
62	
63	describe("cooking new meats", () => {
64	  test("raw beef smelts into cooked beef with coal at a furnace", () => {
65	    const r = recipe("cook_beef");
66	    expect(r.station).toBe("furnace");
67	    const slots = inventory([
68	      ["raw_beef", 1],
69	      ["coal", 1]
70	    ]);
71	    expect(canCraft(slots, r)).toBe(true);
72	    const next = craft(slots, r);
73	    expect(next).not.toBeNull();
74	    expect(countsById(next!).get("cooked_beef")).toBe(1);
75	  });
76	
77	  test("raw porkchop smelts into cooked porkchop with coal at a furnace", () => {
78	    const r = recipe("cook_porkchop");
79	    expect(r.station).toBe("furnace");
80	    const slots = inventory([
81	      ["raw_porkchop", 1],
82	      ["coal", 1]
83	    ]);
84	    expect(canCraft(slots, r)).toBe(true);
85	    const next = craft(slots, r);
86	    expect(next).not.toBeNull();
87	    expect(countsById(next!).get("cooked_porkchop")).toBe(1);
88	  });
89	});
90	
91	describe("coal & fuel economy", () => {
92	  test("charcoal smelts from a single wood at a furnace", () => {
93	    const r = recipe("charcoal");
94	    expect(r.station).toBe("furnace");
95	    const slots = inventory([["wood", 1]]);
96	    expect(canCraft(slots, r)).toBe(true);
97	    expect(countsById(craft(slots, r)!).get("charcoal")).toBe(1);
98	  });
99	
100	  test("cooking accepts charcoal as an alternative fuel", () => {
101	    const slots = inventory([
102	      ["raw_chicken", 1],
103	      ["charcoal", 1]
104	    ]);
105	    const r = recipe("cook_chicken_charcoal");
106	    expect(canCraft(slots, r)).toBe(true);
107	    expect(countsById(craft(slots, r)!).get("cooked_chicken")).toBe(1);
108	  });
109	
110	  test("TNT crafts from gunpowder and sand", () => {
111	    const r = recipe("tnt");
112	    const slots = inventory([
113	      ["gunpowder", 4],
114	      ["sand", 1]
115	    ]);
116	    expect(canCraft(slots, r)).toBe(true);

142	          ]),

146	    ).toBe(4);
147	  });
148	});

1	import { describe, expect, mock, test } from "bun:test";
2	import { render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
3	import userEvent from "@testing-library/user-event";
4	import InventoryPanel from "@/components/game/InventoryPanel";
5	import { CHEST_SLOTS, HOTBAR_SLOTS, INVENTORY_SLOTS } from "@/lib/game/config";
6	import { CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE } from "@/lib/game/engine/commands";
7	import { createEmptyArmorEquipment, createEmptySlot, createSlot } from "@/lib/game/items";
8	import { RECIPES } from "@/lib/game/recipes";
9	import type { InventorySlot } from "@/lib/game/types";
10	
11	function makeInventory(...items: Array<[string, number] | null>): InventorySlot[] {
12	  const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
13	  items.forEach((item, i) => {
14	    if (item) slots[i] = createSlot(item[0], item[1]);
15	  });
16	  return slots;
17	}
18	
19	function renderPanel(overrides: Partial<Parameters<typeof InventoryPanel>[0]> = {}) {
20	  const props = {
21	    inventory: makeInventory(["dirt", 10], ["stone", 5], ["helmet", 1]),
22	    equippedArmor: createEmptyArmorEquipment(),
23	    selectedHotbarSlot: 0,
24	    hotbarSlots: HOTBAR_SLOTS,
25	    recipes: RECIPES,
26	    craftingStation: null as "furnace" | null,
27	    container: null as InventorySlot[] | null,
28	    canCraft: () => true,
29	    onSwapSlots: mock(),
30	    onMoveStack: mock(),
31	    onToggleEquipArmor: mock(),
32	    onCraft: mock(),
33	    ...overrides
34	  };
35	  render(<InventoryPanel {...props} />);
36	  return props;
37	}
38	
39	function slotButtons() {
40	  // Inventory slot wells, excluding the armor column and recipe entries.
41	  return screen.getAllByRole("button").filter((button) => button.className.includes("inv-slot") && !button.className.includes("armor-slot"));
42	}
43	
44	describe("InventoryPanel", () => {
45	  test("renders all inventory slots plus recipe buttons", () => {
46	    renderPanel();
47	    expect(slotButtons()).toHaveLength(INVENTORY_SLOTS);
48	    expect(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "2 Wood -> 4 Planks" })).toBeTruthy();
49	  });
50	
51	  test("slots are ordered storage first, hotbar row last, with icons and counts", () => {
52	    renderPanel();
53	    const slots = slotButtons();
54	    // DOM order: the 27 storage slots (indices 9..35) render above the hotbar row (0..8).
55	    expect(slots[27].getAttribute("aria-label")).toBe("Slot 1: Dirt");
56	    // Dirt appears as the slot icon and again inside recipe ingredients.
57	    expect(screen.getAllByAltText("Dirt").length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
58	    expect(screen.getByText("10")).toBeTruthy(); // the dirt stack count
59	  });
60	
61	  test("clicking two slots swaps them via onSwapSlots", async () => {
62	    const user = userEvent.setup();
63	    const props = renderPanel();
64	    const slots = slotButtons();
65	
66	    await user.click(slots[0]); // pending — first storage slot, inventory index 9
67	    expect(slots[0].className).toContain("pending");
68	    await user.click(slots[1]);
69	    expect(props.onSwapSlots).toHaveBeenCalledWith(9, 10);
70	  });
71	
72	  test("clicking the same slot twice cancels the pending selection", async () => {
73	    const user = userEvent.setup();
74	    const props = renderPanel();
75	    const slots = slotButtons();
76	
77	    await user.click(slots[0]);
78	    await user.click(slots[0]);
79	    expect(slots[0].className).not.toContain("pending");
80	    expect(props.onSwapSlots).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
81	
82	    await user.click(slots[1]); // a fresh click starts a new pending selection
83	    expect(props.onSwapSlots).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
84	  });
85

87	    const user = userEvent.setup();

89	    const slots = slotButtons();
90	
91	    await user.click(slots[27 + 2]); // the helmet sits in hotbar slot 2

93	    expect(props.onSwapSlots).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
94	  });

99	    const helmetSlot = screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Helmet: Helmet" });
100	    expect(helmetSlot.className).toContain("filled");

105	  });
106

108	    const user = userEvent.setup();

1	import * as THREE from "three";
2	import { BlockId, type WorldType } from "@/lib/world";
3	
4	export type ItemKind = "block" | "weapon" | "tool" | "armor" | "food" | "material";
5	export type ArmorSlot = "helmet" | "face_mask" | "neck_protection" | "chestplate" | "leggings" | "boots";
6	export type EquippedArmor = Record<ArmorSlot, string | null>;
7	
8	export type ItemDef = {
9	  id: string;
10	  label: string;
11	  kind: ItemKind;
12	  blockId?: BlockId;
13	  attack?: number;
14	  meleeReach?: number;
15	  throwDamage?: number;
16	  minePower?: number;
17	  mineTier?: number;
18	  armorSlot?: ArmorSlot;
19	  defense?: number;
20	  maxDurability?: number;
21	  /** Hunger points restored when eaten (food items only). */
22	  hunger?: number;
23	};
24	
25	export type InventorySlot = {
26	  id: string | null;
27	  label: string;
28	  kind: ItemKind | null;
29	  count: number;
30	  blockId?: BlockId;
31	  attack?: number;
32	  meleeReach?: number;
33	  throwDamage?: number;
34	  minePower?: number;
35	  mineTier?: number;
36	  armorSlot?: ArmorSlot;
37	  defense?: number;
38	  durability?: number;
39	  maxDurability?: number;
40	  hunger?: number;
41	};
42	
43	export type Recipe = {
44	  id: string;
45	  label: string;
46	  cost: Array<{ slotId: string; count: number }>;
47	  result: { slotId: string; count: number };
48	  /**
49	   * Station required for this recipe; omitted means the basic crafting grid.
50	   * "furnace" smelts; "villager" is a trade offer, unlocked while trading with a villager.
51	   */
52	  station?: "furnace" | "villager";
53	};
54	
55	export type MobKind = "sheep" | "chicken" | "horse" | "cow" | "pig" | "zombie" | "skeleton" | "spider" | "creeper" | "villager" | "boss";
56	
57	export type MobModel = {
58	  group: THREE.Group;
59	  legs: THREE.Mesh[];
60	  halfHeight: number;
61	  materials: THREE.Material[];
62	  geometries: THREE.BufferGeometry[];
63	};
64	
65	/** A persisted inventory/container slot: just enough to rebuild it on load. */
66	export type SavedSlot = { id: string | null; count: number; durability?: number };
67	
68	/** A block-entity: a placed chest's contents, keyed by its voxel index. */
69	export type SavedContainer = { index: number; slots: SavedSlot[] };
70	
71	/** Legacy save shape (40 inventory slots, 10-slot hotbar) — accepted and migrated on load. */
72	export type SaveDataV1 = {
73	  version: 1;
74	  seed: number;
75	  changes: Array<[number, number]>;
76	  inventoryCounts?: Record<string, number>;
77	  inventorySlots?: SavedSlot[];
78	  equippedArmor?: Partial<EquippedArmor>;
79	  selectedSlot: number;
80	  player: { x: number; y: number; z: number };
81	};
82	
83	/** v2 save shape: same fields as v1, reinterpreted for 36 slots / 9-slot hotbar. */
84	export type SaveDataV2 = Omit<SaveDataV1, "version"> & { version: 2 };
85	
86	/**
87	 * v3 save shape: v2 plus persisted time-of-day, player stats, and the bed
88	 * respawn point. All added fields are optional so the v2→v3 migration is a pure
89	 * version bump and pre-v3 saves load with sensible defaults.
90	 */
91	export type SaveDataV3 = Omit<SaveDataV2, "version"> & {
92	  version: 3;
93	  dayClock?: number;
94	  hearts?: number;
95	  hunger?: number;
96	  spawnPoint?: { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null;
97	};
98	
99	/**
100	 * v4 save shape: v3 plus chest contents as block-entities. The field is
101	 * optional so the v3→v4 migration is a pure version bump and pre-v4 saves load
102	 * with no containers.
103	 */
104	export type SaveDataV4 = Omit<SaveDataV3, "version"> & {
105	  version: 4;
106	  blockEntities?: SavedContainer[];
107	};
108	
109	/**
110	 * Current save shape (v5): v4 plus the set of dungeon loot-chest voxel indices
111	 * the player has already opened or broken. Dungeon chests are filled lazily on
112	 * first access and this set — not the chest's emptiness — is what prevents a
113	 * re-roll on reload (an emptied chest is dropped from `blockEntities`). The
114	 * field is optional so the v4→v5 migration is a pure version bump. Note
115	 * `SAVE_KEY` is bumped to v6 alongside this because the dungeon worldgen
116	 * changed the deterministic block-diff baseline.
117	 */
118	export type SaveData = Omit<SaveDataV4, "version"> & {
119	  version: 5;
120	  lootedChests?: number[];
121	  /** Generation preset; absent ⇒ "default" (pre-feature and legacy saves). Like `seed`, fixed for the world's life. */
122	  worldType?: WorldType;
123	};
124

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts",
  "old_string": "// Villager trades are station-gated recipes, so they share the recipe book + the\n// `craft` command. They live in their own module (the trade analog of recipes).\nexport const RECIPES: Recipe[] = [...CRAFTING_RECIPES, ...TRADES];",
  "new_string": "// Villager trades are station-gated recipes, so they share the recipe book + the\n// `craft` command. They live in their own module (the trade analog of recipes).\nexport const RECIPES: Recipe[] = [...CRAFTING_RECIPES, ...TRADES];\n\n// --- Recipe categories (recipe-book grouping) ---\n\n/**\n * The section a recipe falls under in the recipe book. Station recipes get their\n * own group (a furnace smelts, a villager trades); everything else is grouped by\n * the kind of item it produces.\n */\nexport type RecipeCategory = \"Tools\" | \"Weapons\" | \"Armor\" | \"Building\" | \"Food\" | \"Materials\" | \"Smelting\" | \"Trades\";\n\n/** Fixed display order; `groupRecipes` emits sections in this sequence. */\nexport const RECIPE_CATEGORY_ORDER: RecipeCategory[] = [\"Tools\", \"Weapons\", \"Armor\", \"Building\", \"Food\", \"Materials\", \"Smelting\", \"Trades\"];\n\nconst KIND_TO_CATEGORY: Record<ItemKind, RecipeCategory> = {\n  tool: \"Tools\",\n  weapon: \"Weapons\",\n  armor: \"Armor\",\n  block: \"Building\",\n  food: \"Food\",\n  material: \"Materials\"\n};\n\n/**\n * The recipe book section for a recipe. Station recipes take precedence so\n * smelting and trades stay together; otherwise the category is derived from the\n * result item's `kind` (falling back to \"Materials\" for an unknown result).\n */\nexport function recipeCategory(recipe: Recipe): RecipeCategory {\n  if (recipe.station === \"villager\") return \"Trades\";\n  if (recipe.station === \"furnace\") return \"Smelting\";\n  const kind = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[recipe.result.slotId]?.kind;\n  return kind ? KIND_TO_CATEGORY[kind] : \"Materials\";\n}\n\nexport type RecipeGroup = { category: RecipeCategory; recipes: Recipe[] };\n\n/**\n * Buckets recipes into the fixed category order, dropping empty categories.\n * Within each group, recipes the player can make right now (`canMakeNow`) are\n * listed first; both partitions preserve the original source order.\n */\nexport function groupRecipes(recipes: Recipe[], canMakeNow: (recipe: Recipe) => boolean): RecipeGroup[] {\n  const buckets = new Map<RecipeCategory, Recipe[]>();\n  for (const recipe of recipes) {\n    const category = recipeCategory(recipe);\n    const bucket = buckets.get(category);\n    if (bucket) bucket.push(recipe);\n    else buckets.set(category, [recipe]);\n  }\n\n  const groups: RecipeGroup[] = [];\n  for (const category of RECIPE_CATEGORY_ORDER) {\n    const bucket = buckets.get(category);\n    if (!bucket) continue;\n    const ready: Recipe[] = [];\n    const rest: Recipe[] = [];\n    for (const recipe of bucket) (canMakeNow(recipe) ? ready : rest).push(recipe);\n    groups.push({ category, recipes: [...ready, ...rest] });\n  }\n  return groups;\n}"
}

218	good (it's tough and drops nothing). Grab the loot, deal with the mob, and leave,
219	or clear the spawner first if you want to loot in peace.
220	
221	## Crafting
222	
223	Open the inventory (`I`) to reach the crafting panel. Pick a recipe whose
224	ingredients you have and craft it — the result drops into your inventory (crafting
225	is refused if there's no room rather than destroying the overflow). Stacks hold up
226	to **99** items; tools, weapons, and armor take one slot each and show durability.
227	
228	The basic recipe grid is always available. **Smelting** recipes (cooking raw meat)
229	require a **furnace**: craft one from 8 cobble, place it, and right-click it to open
230	the panel in furnace mode — the cooking recipes unlock while a furnace is open and
231	show as "Requires Furnace" otherwise.
232	
233	See the full list of **45 recipes** in the [reference](reference.md#recipes).
234	
235	## Doors
236	
237	Craft a **wood door** from **6 planks** and place it on a solid floor with two
238	clear blocks above it. A door is a thin, two-block-tall panel: right-click either
239	half to open or close the whole door. Closed doors block players and mobs; open
240	doors rotate against their hinge so you can pass. Mobs cannot open or close doors.
241	Breaking either half removes the whole door and returns one door item.
242	
243	## Storage
244	
245	Your pack only holds 36 slots, so build **chests** to stash the overflow. Craft a
246	chest from **8 planks**, place it, and **right-click** it to open it: a **27-slot**
247	grid appears above your inventory. Move items the same way you rearrange the
248	inventory — **click a slot, then click where it should go** — across either grid.
249	Close the panel (`I` or `Esc`) and the chest keeps its contents, which are **saved
250	with your world**.
251	
252	Breaking a chest doesn't destroy what's inside: its contents **spill back into your
253	inventory** (and you get the chest item back). If your inventory is too full to hold
254	everything, the break is refused and the chest stays put — empty it a bit first.
255	
256	## Mobs and breeding
257

1	# Gameplay Reference
2	
3	Scannable tables for everything in the game: recipes, blocks, mobs, and item
4	stats. For how it all fits together, read the [player manual](manual.md).
5	
6	> **The code is the source of truth.** These tables are a convenience snapshot of
7	> `lib/game/recipes.ts`, `lib/game/items.ts`, `lib/world/blocks.ts`,
8	> `lib/game/mobs.ts`, and `lib/game/mobLoot.ts`. If a number here disagrees with
9	> the game, the code wins — and this page needs an update.
10	
11	## World types
12	
13	Chosen when you create a world (alongside the name and seed) and fixed for its
14	life. All four share the same five biomes — they reshape terrain, not the biome
15	mix. Source: `lib/world/worldTypes.ts` + `terrainConfigFor` in `lib/world/generation.ts`.
16	
17	| Type      | Terrain                                                                                     |
18	| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
19	| Default   | Balanced terrain with every biome — the original generator                                  |
20	| Superflat | Level ground at a fixed height; a builder's canvas (caves and ores still generate below)    |
21	| Amplified | Exaggerated relief — towering peaks and deep valleys                                        |
22	| Islands   | A raised sea breaks the land into scattered, gently sloped islands with deep ocean channels |
23	
24	## Recipes
25	
26	**54 crafting recipes** (plus **10 villager trades**, listed under [Trading](#trading)).
27	All use the always-available crafting grid except the nine **furnace** smelting
28	recipes, which need an open furnace.
29	
30	### Building & materials
31	
32	| Result      | Ingredients          |
33	| ----------- | -------------------- |
34	| 4 Planks    | 2 Wood               |
35	| 2 Glass     | 4 Sand               |
36	| 2 Brick     | 2 Dirt + 2 Stone     |
37	| 1 Furnace   | 8 Cobble             |
38	| 1 Chest     | 8 Planks             |
39	| 1 Wood Door | 6 Planks             |
40	| 4 Torch     | 1 Coal + 1 Wood      |

1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
4	
5	## [Unreleased]
6	
7	### Added
8	
9	- **Villagers & trading**: a passive NPC you can barter with gives gathering an economy, all on **zero new assets** (procedural model + a recolored gem sprite)
10	  - **Villager** mob: passive like the animals but **doesn't flee** — walk up and **right-click to trade**. It drops nothing if killed (don't murder your shopkeeper) and spawns into the day-one passive population near you
11	  - **Trading**: the currency is the **emerald**. Right-clicking a villager opens its offers in the inventory's recipe book (re-titled **Trading**). The economy is two-sided — **sell** gathered materials (wheat, coal, leather, gold ore) for emeralds, then **spend** them on goods (bread, torches, arrows, a stone pickaxe, sliver/ruby ore). No use caps; trading is bounded only by what you can gather
12	  - **Architecture**: a trade is simply a **`station: "villager"` recipe** (`lib/game/trades.ts`, spread into `RECIPES`), so it reuses the entire crafting pipeline and recipe-book UI — opening a villager just sets `craftingStation = "villager"`, and the existing `craft` command runs the trade. The new `"villager"` `MobKind` fills all five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables. **No save-format or worldgen change**: villagers are transient, the open trade station is session-only, there are no per-trade use caps to persist, and emerald/traded items save by string id. (Village _structures_ are a possible future addition — villagers currently wander the world among the existing houses rather than spawning in generated settlements, which keeps this change off the worldgen-determinism baseline.)
13	- **Creepers, TNT & explosions**: the game gains its first explosion system — a sneaking mob that detonates and a craftable demolition charge — all on **zero new assets** (procedural model/atlas + synthesized boom)
14	  - **Explosion primitive** (`lib/game/engine/systems/explosion.ts`): `explode()` destroys blocks in a power-radius sphere (distance falloff vs each block's blast resistance, sparing bedrock/spawner/lava), then damages the player and mobs out to twice that radius with the same falloff, applies knockback, and emits one `explosion` event. It is **not a new subsystem** — it batches over the existing `blockChanges.set` chokepoint (each cell relights locally, records the save delta) and sets `worldMeshDirty` **once**, so a whole crater costs a single remesh. It only lowers mob hp (never splices), so it is safe to run mid mob-tick; the caller sweeps the dead
15	  - **Creeper**: a hostile mob that chases silently, then lights a ~1.5 s fuse within ~2.6 blocks (hissing and swelling white), and **explodes** — dying in its own blast and dropping **gunpowder**. Walk past it while primed and the fuse aborts; kill it first to take the gunpowder safely. Burns in daylight like a zombie
16	  - **TNT**: craft it (4 gunpowder + 1 sand), place it like a block, and **right-click it with a torch** to light a fuse (the torch isn't consumed). It detonates after a delay and **chains** adjacent TNT. Lit fuses are session-only (`state.primedTnt`); a save written mid-fuse reloads with the TNT inert
17	  - **Minimum hostile spawn distance** (`HOSTILE_SPAWN_MIN_RADIUS`, 16): every hostile spawn (initial + night trickle) now keeps a standoff from the player, so nothing — least of all a creeper — can materialize point-blank
18	  - The new `"creeper"` `MobKind` fills all five exhaustive `Record<MobKind>` tables. Explosions drive a procedural fireball + smoke burst and a synthesized boom; a primed creeper swells and flashes via its `MobState.fuseTimer`. **No save-format or worldgen change**: creepers are spawned, TNT is crafted, fuses are transient, and the new items save by string id
19	- **Coal & a fuel economy**: a new shallow ore deepens the early game and gives the furnace a real fuel. **Coal ore** is the most common ore (large veins reaching near the surface, mineable with a wood pickaxe) and drops the **coal** fuel item directly. **Charcoal** smelts from a single log as a coal substitute, so a player who hasn't struck coal can still cook. Both fuel the furnace's cooking recipes (chicken/mutton/beef/porkchop) and **torches now cost coal + wood** instead of wood alone. All procedural, **zero new assets** (a dark-flecked atlas tile + a recolored sprite lump)
20	  - Coal is placed in its own pass (`placeCoal`) on a **dedicated PRNG**, so it never consumes from the shared worldgen `rand` stream — every other ore, tree, and structure stays **byte-identical** to a coal-free world, exactly as deep-cave lava did. Only underground coal cells differ; the meshing and structural-probe tests stay green and only the byte hashes were re-rolled
21	  - **Worldgen re-baseline**: the new ore changes the deterministic terrain baseline, so **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` is bumped `7` → `8`** — a world whose recorded version differs discards its stale block-diffs and reboots from its seed. The save **schema** stays version 5 (coal/charcoal are additive items, coal ore persists in the block diff). `canMineBlock` gates coal behind a wood pickaxe (tier 1, like stone); `GEN.coalConfig` holds its vein tunables
22	- **Cow & pig farm animals**: two new passive mobs join sheep/chicken/horse, following the same template. **Cows** drop leather + raw beef, **pigs** drop raw porkchop; both raw meats smelt to a cooked form (hunger 8) at a furnace and breed like the others — feed a cow **wheat**, a pig **seeds**. Procedural box models + synthesized moo/grunt calls, **zero new assets**. Mobs and breeding are session-only, the new foods save by string id, so **no save-format or worldgen change**
23	- **World types**: when creating a world you now pick a generation preset, not just a seed — **Default**, **Superflat** (level builder's canvas), **Amplified** (towering, dramatic relief), or **Islands** (scattered land in a raised sea). Each generates genuinely different terrain over the same biome map; **zero new assets** (the picker reuses the menu chrome)
24	  - `generateWorld(world, worldType)` forks on the type before any block write via `terrainConfigFor` (`lib/world/generation.ts`), varying only sea level and per-biome surface height; caves, ores, lava, trees, structures, and dungeons are unchanged. The **Default path is byte-identical** to before — existing worlds and the determinism hashes are untouched, so **no `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump**. Each new type is pinned by its own SHA-256 baseline plus structural probes (flat is level, amplified spans a wider band, islands floods more of the map while still leaving a dry spawn)
25	  - The chosen type is stored on `WorldMeta` and in the `SaveData` blob (like `seed`), so a world always regenerates with the type its block-diffs were recorded against. `WorldType` lives in `lib/world/worldTypes.ts`; the schema stays at version 5 (the field is optional, absent ⇒ default)
26	- **Multiple worlds & player profiles**: the game now opens to a menu instead of dropping straight into one fixed world. A **profile** (name + skin) owns a list of named **worlds**, like user accounts on a shared console — pick a profile, then pick or create a world to play. All client-side in localStorage; **zero new assets** (the menus reuse the existing Minecraft-style chrome and generated skin portraits)
27	  - **Two-level save hierarchy** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`, `lib/game/worlds.ts`): a versioned `minecraft_profiles_v1` manifest (`{ id, name, skinId, createdAt }` + `activeProfileId`) and a `minecraft_worlds_v1` index (`{ id, profileId, name, seed, worldgenVersion, createdAt, lastPlayedAt }`), with each world's `SaveData` in its own `minecraft_world_save_<id>` key. Readers are total — malformed entries are dropped and a dangling active profile is repaired. New-world seeds are resolved once at creation (blank → random, numeric → clamped, other text → stable FNV hash). The skin moved off the old global `minecraft_skin_v1` key onto the profile; **audio settings stay global**
28	  - **Menu shell** (`components/GameShell.tsx`, `components/menu/*`): a profile-select → world-select → play state machine with create / rename / delete on both levels (deleting a profile cascades its worlds and their save blobs). Switching worlds, Load, or Reset remounts the game subtree via a React `key`, so the engine/renderer are disposed and re-created **without a page reload**. The tab remembers its active world in `sessionStorage`, so a reload resumes straight back into play while a fresh tab cold-starts at the menu. The pause menu gains **Save & Quit to Worlds**
29	  - **Per-world worldgen versioning**: each world records the `WORLDGEN_VERSION` it was generated under (`lib/game/config.ts`). A future worldgen change bumps that constant and discards only the affected worlds' stale block-diffs (rebooting them from their seed) — replacing the old whole-store `SAVE_KEY` rename, which reset every world. The save _schema_ is unchanged (still version 5)
30	  - **First run & migration** (`lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`): a brand-new player is greeted by the create-profile form (name + skin) — their first act is making a profile. An existing single-world save is instead folded into a default "Player" profile (carrying the old skin) plus one "My World"; the legacy blob is copied before the old key is removed, so a failure can't lose data
31	
32	## [0.8.0] - 2026-06-14
33	
34	### Changed
35	
36	- Starting play now requires a double-click to acquire pointer lock, preventing an accidental single click from immediately capturing the mouse. No save-format or worldgen impact.
37	- Emergency unstuck now requires **`Shift` + `U`** instead of a bare `U`, so a stray keypress can no longer teleport the player mid-play. The automatic unstuck safeguard (which fires when wedged in terrain or falling below the world) is unchanged. No save-format or worldgen impact.
38	- Placed glass now renders as a clear, low-reflection transparent layer. Internal faces between adjacent glass blocks are culled, while neighboring opaque block faces remain visible through it. No save-format or worldgen impact.
39	
40	### Added
```

Done. **PR #24 is open:** https://github.com/hutusi/monecraft/pull/24

Summary of what shipped:

- **Branch** `feat/recipe-book-categories` → 3 focused commits (helper + tests, UI render + CSS + tests, docs/changelog), pushed and PR'd against `hutusi/main`.
- **The change:** the recipe book now renders **labeled sections** — Tools, Weapons, Armor, Building, Food, Materials, then station-gated Smelting and Trades — with **craftable-now recipes floated to the top** of each group. Categories are derived from the existing item `kind`, so nothing can drift and adding a recipe needs no extra metadata. Pure presentation: no save/worldgen/recipe-data impact.
- **Verify gate green:** lint, typecheck, format:check, `bun test` (547 pass / 0 fail), and the production build all pass.

To answer your very first question directly: yes — grouping was the right call here. The list was long and repetitive, the data already carried the grouping signal, and it matches modern Minecraft's category-tabbed recipe book. The one judgment call I made for you was giving the furnace/villager recipes their own Smelting/Trades sections instead of scattering them, which keeps the kind-based groups clean.

1	# Group recipes by category in the crafting recipe book
2	
3	## Context
4	
5	The recipe book (right column of the survival inventory) renders **every recipe as one
6	flat, unlabeled scrolling list** — and because the full `RECIPES` array is passed in and
7	station-locked recipes are merely dimmed rather than hidden, that's ~64 rows at the base
8	crafting table (45 craftable + 9 furnace + 10 villager, all visible). It's hard to scan
9	for two reasons: it's long, and it's repetitive (7 tiers each of pickaxe/sword/spear = 21
10	near-identical rows that blur together).
11	
12	The data already carries everything needed to fix this: every result item has a `kind`
13	(`block | weapon | tool | armor | food | material`) in `lib/game/items.ts`, and
14	`recipes.ts` is already loosely ordered by category in source. This change makes that
15	implicit structure visible — **category section headers** within the same scrolling list,
16	with **craftable-now recipes floated to the top of each group**. It's purely a UI
17	presentation change (no save/worldgen/config impact) and mirrors modern Minecraft's
18	category-tabbed recipe book.
19	
20	Decisions locked with the user:

## AUDIT SUMMARY

Based on my comprehensive analysis, here are the concrete findings:

### 1. STALE COUNTS IN DOCS

**Critical mismatches:**

- **`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/manual.md` line 238**: Claims "45 recipes" but the code has 54 crafting recipes (plus 10 trades = 64 total). The reference.md correctly states "54 crafting recipes (plus 10 villager trades)" at line 26, so manual.md is out of sync.

- **`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/reference.md` line 118**: Claims "34 block types (plus air)" but the table lists only 32 unique block types (Grass through Water). However, the actual `BlockId` enum in the code has 49 non-air blocks. The reference table doesn't include door variants (16 block IDs for the different door states) — they're documented separately in the code but not as individual rows. The headline "34" doesn't match the table's "32" rows.

### 2. TEST COVERAGE MAP DRIFT

**Tests documented in `docs/testing.md` but not found on disk:**
- None detected. All tests listed in the map exist.

**Tests found on disk but NOT in the coverage map** (6 additions post-documentation):

All six of these are legitimate and should be added to the testing.md table.

### 3. CHANGELOG STATE

**`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/CHANGELOG.md` lines 5-35**: There is a substantial **[Unreleased]** section documenting:

## Approved Plan:
# Prep the fork for an upstream contribution: doc-drift cleanup + cut v0.9.0

## Context

The fork `hutusi/monecraft` is **29 commits ahead of upstream `davidhu0527/monecraft` (0 behind)** —
a whole batch of features (coal/fuel economy, creepers/TNT/explosions, cows/pigs, villagers/trading,
world types, multiple worlds & player profiles, recipe-book category grouping) that aren't upstream yet.
Before opening an upstream sync PR, we want the docs, the test-coverage map, and the release metadata to
be clean — "code is the source of truth" is a documented principle here, so stale counts are exactly what
a reviewer flags.

Audit finding: **no new tests or doc *content* are actually missing** — every feature shipped with its
tests and docs. What exists is **drift**: two stale hardcoded counts, a test-coverage map that lags the
actual test files by six, and a large `CHANGELOG [Unreleased]` section that was never cut to a version
(`package.json` is still `0.8.0`).

Decisions locked with the user:
- **Cut v0.9.0** as part of this (CHANGELOG stamp + `package.json` bump).
- **Prep only** — do NOT open the upstream PR; the user will open it. (Also do not push; commit locally
  so the user reviews first, per their standing preference.)

## Tasks

### 1. Fix stale doc counts

- **`docs/manual.md:238`** — "the full list of **45 recipes**" is stale. Change to match the verified
  count (`reference.md` says **54 crafting recipes**). Verify first: `grep -c "id:" lib/game/recipes.ts`
  against `CRAFTING_RECIPES`, and `TRADES` in `lib/game/trades.ts`; use the confirmed crafting count.
- **`docs/reference.md:118`** — "**34 block types** (plus air)" disagrees with its own table (~32 rows)
  and the code. Reconcile carefully: read the full Blocks table, count its rows, and cross-check against
  the placeable/visible blocks in `lib/world/blocks.ts` (`BlockId` / `BLOCK_COLORS`, excluding state
  variants like door/wheat stages). Set the headline to the true row count, and add any genuinely missing
  block rows (e.g. confirm coal ore / TNT / mossy cobble / spawner are represented). Don't guess the
  number — derive it.

### 2. Refresh the test-coverage map — `docs/testing.md`

Add the six test files that exist on disk but aren't in the "What is covered" table. Read each briefly to
write an accurate one-line "what it pins down", then either add a row or fold into the closest existing row:
- `components/game/ItemTooltip.test.tsx` → Components row
- `lib/world/lighting.test.ts` → new row (per-voxel lighting)
- `lib/game/mobLoot.test.ts` → mob-drops row
- `lib/game/input/inputController.test.ts` → new row (input layer)
- `lib/game/engine/systems/breeding.test.ts` → new/Simulation row
- `lib/game/engine/systems/randomTicks.test.ts` → new/Simulation row (crop growth)

(Markdown tables here are column-padded — run `bunx prettier --write docs/testing.md` after editing.)

### 3. Cut release v0.9.0

- **`CHANGELOG.md`** — rename `## [Unreleased]` to `## [0.9.0] - 2026-06-14`, and add a fresh empty
  `## [Unreleased]` above it (Keep a Changelog convention). The recipe-grouping `### Changed` entry and the
  big `### Added` list move into 0.9.0 as-is.
- **`package.json:3`** — `"version": "0.8.0"` → `"0.9.0"`.
- Sanity: `grep -rn "0\.8\.0"` across the repo (excluding CHANGELOG history + lockfiles) to catch any other
  app-version reference that should bump. The save-schema version (5) is unrelated — do not touch it.

### 4. Side cleanup (not a repo change)

Update the stale auto-memory `roadmap-candidates.md`: upstream PR **davidhu0527#7 is merged, not open**
(verified via `gh`). Fork is 29 ahead / 0 behind upstream with no open upstream PR.

## Commits (local only, straight to `main`)

This is low-risk housekeeping (docs + changelog + version), single-purpose — per AGENTS.md it commits to
`main` rather than a feature branch. The cleanup must live on `main` anyway, since the upstream sync PR
diffs `upstream:main...hutusi:main`. Two focused slices, Conventional Commits, no `Co-Authored-By`:
- **Commit 1** — `docs: correct stale counts and refresh the test-coverage map`
  (`docs/manual.md`, `docs/reference.md`, `docs/testing.md`).
- **Commit 2** — `chore(release): v0.9.0` (`CHANGELOG.md`, `package.json`).

Do **not** push and do **not** open any PR (fork or upstream) — leave that to the user.

## Verification

- `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun test`, `bun run build` — the CI
  `verify` gate (docs-only + a one-line package.json change shouldn't move code, but run it to be safe).
- Re-grep to confirm no remaining stale `45 recipes` / mismatched block count / `0.8.0` app-version string.
- Report the commits and leave the branch clean; remind the user the upstream sync PR (one combined
  `gh pr create --repo davidhu0527/monecraft --base main`) is theirs to open when ready.

---- enum def ----
export const enum BlockId {
  Air = 0,
  Grass = 1,
  Dirt = 2,
  Stone = 3,
  Wood = 4,
  Leaves = 5,
  Bedrock = 6,
  Planks = 7,
  Cobblestone = 8,
  Sand = 9,
  Brick = 10,
  Glass = 11,
  SliverOre = 12,
  RubyOre = 13,
  GoldOre = 14,
  SapphireOre = 15,
  DiamondOre = 16,
  Water = 17,
  Snow = 18,
  Cactus = 19,
  Bed = 20,
  Farmland = 21,
  // Wheat growth stages — consecutive ids so a crop advances by +1 (see randomTicks.ts).
  WheatStage0 = 22,
  WheatStage1 = 23,
  WheatStage2 = 24,
  WheatStage3 = 25,
  Furnace = 26,
  Chest = 27,
  // Dungeon blocks (worldgen-only flavor + the spawner block-entity marker).
  MossyCobblestone = 28,
  Spawner = 29,
  // Wood doors — 16 contiguous ids (4 facings × open/closed × lower/upper).
  // doors.ts derives state by offset from DoorNorthLower, so keep them in order.
  DoorNorthLower = 30,
  DoorNorthUpper = 31,
  DoorEastLower = 32,
  DoorEastUpper = 33,
  DoorSouthLower = 34,
  DoorSouthUpper = 35,
  DoorWestLower = 36,
  DoorWestUpper = 37,
  DoorNorthOpenLower = 38,
  DoorNorthOpenUpper = 39,
  DoorEastOpenLower = 40,
  DoorEastOpenUpper = 41,
  DoorSouthOpenLower = 42,
  DoorSouthOpenUpper = 43,
  DoorWestOpenLower = 44,
  DoorWestOpenUpper = 45,
  // A placeable light source: a solid block that emits block light (see lighting.ts).
  Torch = 46,
  // A worldgen-only hazard: a solid, unmineable block that emits max block light
  // and burns the player on contact (see lighting.ts, playerStats.ts).
  Lava = 47,
  // A shallow, common ore: mineable with a wood pickaxe, drops coal (furnace fuel).
  CoalOre = 48,
  // A placeable explosive: right-click with a torch to ignite a fuse, then it blows.
  Tnt = 49
}
